Even Helen’s voice rang in your head, ‘you need to let them in and allow them to shoulder that fear with you.’ Oh but you were so scared to do that. Even with the promise that they wouldn’t look at you differently, you were still scared. So when dinner came around, you didn’t engage in the conversation as they asked Bruce how Wakanda was. Instead, you pushed your food around your plate to lost in your own thoughts. Once dinner was put away, Tony ordered ice cream for everyone. It was suppose to be a fun team bonding experience but the ice cream made your stomach twist as you listened to the team talk and laugh. “Hey,” America whispered, pumping your shoulder against yours. “Are you okay?” You sighed, not meaning it to be loud enough to grab the attention of other other Avengers. You swirled your spoon in your ice cream so the colors were mixed together. “I,” you sighed again, feeling angry with yourself for not finding the correct words. “I want to tell all of you about what happened to me, if that’s okay?” The mood of the group shifted and you felt a little bad but you needed to do this now before you lost the courage. “Of course, " Wanda smiled. “Take your time.” You crossed your legs and focused on your ice cream.

    “I was born with this ability,” you started. You knew some of them knew that but you thought it was good to start from the beginning. Dropping your spoon, electricity danced around your fingers. “They didn’t surface until I was about 4 but even then my parents made it clear they wanted nothing to do with me,” you took another spoonful of your ice cream, hand shaking slightly. “They didn’t want a second kid and my mother didn’t know she was pregnant until a friend told her. He claimed God spoke to him through a dream and told him of the pregnancy. But I wasn’t going to be a gift, I was going to be a curse. That not even the Devil wanted me.” You heard Natasha scuff. You smiled at the sound, imagining the red head rolled her eyes and Wanda quietly having to tell her to behave. You kept your eyes trained down. “So they kept me a secret, only a trusted few new about me.”

    “Why didn’t they give you up for adoption?” ate questioned. It was a good one that you asked yourself all the time.

    “I learned quickly that my father enjoyed two things; power and control,” you answered. “There I was a young girl desperate for her father’s love. I did everything I could to get it, no matter how many times he hit me,” you whispered, shaking your head. “Anyways, I accidental shocked my brother. They were furious and locked me in a small room in the basement for 3 days. The only reason I survived was Caleb bring me food and water. He had to be careful too. He was my parents’ favorite but that didn’t always protect him. When they found out I lived, they sent a priest to form an exorcism on me to get the Devil out.” “You were 4 years old,” you heard the anger in every word Yelena said. You nodded. Your father held you down at the priest performed it. It wasn’t the only one you had to endure but they didn’t need to know that.

    “Obviously, it didn’t work,” you chuckled, trying to lighten up the situation. “I remember they made me kneel in the corner and pray to God to save me. I spent hours reading over the bible,” you took another bite of your ice cream. It was melted but it was a good distraction. “That went on for a year or two until they realized I could use my powers to benefit them. I gave power to the church, our home, and those who knew I existed but it was not good enough. Every bad thing that happened I was to blame, someone leaving the church, my father getting into a chair accident, or my brother getting sick. It was all my fault and one day I snapped,” you felt America tangle her hand with your free one, squeezing it tight. You were grateful for it.

    “You don’t have to continue, sweetie,” Pepper said. You smiled at the CEO. You were almost done with this section of your story.

    “Caleb went to school,” you continued. “And my father wouldn’t stop yelling. I don’t remember why he was upset,” your voice began to shake. “The pressure was building and I couldn’t stop it. It became to much and I let go. I woke up to the house around me was burnt to the ground. First responders were on their way and I ran,” you finally looked up at the Black Widow, seeing nothing but warmth in her eyes. “I learned later they said it was an electrical fire but it was me.” A heavy silence fell among the group, it was Tony that broke it. “Look I’ll be the first one to say it since we are all thinking it. They got what they deserved,” Pepper gasped, slapping the billionaire.

Look kid,” he stopped mid sentence. “I have words for wisdom to tell you but I fear my wife so I’ll tell you when we are alone.” You laughed, whipping away a tear that fell. “What I think Mr. Stark is trying to say,” Vision said. “We are very sorry you had to endure that as a child.”

Sometimes you felt so stupid for trusting him. “He promised me a safe place to sleep, food, and that other ids were going to be there. I went with him.”

You nodded, keeping your eyes on the floor.

    “Buck,” Steve said, chasing after the man. “Bucky, hold on.” He finally caught up to him and grabbed onto his arms.     You were fighting to keep your eyes opened as you rested on Kate’s lap with a movie playing. It was a Scooby-Doo movie but you were lost on the plot with sleep clouding your mind. Also, Kate running her fingers trough your hair wasn’t helping. But you were scared to fall asleep. You worried what monsters your mind would create. You were powerless to fight it and fell asleep.

    On either side of you was a guard as you walked down the familiar hallways. They were armed with assault rifles and military armor with orders to shot on sight if you tried to escape. You weren’t going to escape. Every bone in your body hurt. There was a dark hole growing over your hear; accepting your fate here. They opened a door and pushed you in, not entering with you. Inside was a man, long brown hair and blue eyes. He was strapped to a chair, unable to move and no light in his eyes. The most striking part of his appearance was his one metal arm. “Hi little spark,” he stepped out of the corner. “Come here,” you took a few steps and took his out stretched hand. “This man right is very important to us. You can call him Winter.”

    “Winter,” you said slowly but the man didn’t move.

    “But even though he’s important to us doesn’t mean he’s free from punishment. Just like you.” you moved you behind Winter where there was a wooden box. “Up you get,” As you stood on the box, he let go of your hand. “Punish him.” He told you. You had a feeling what he wanted you to do but you couldn’t. Maybe he was mistaken. This man could die. Before you could protest, he grabbed your hands and put them on either side of his head. “I won’t ask again.” You nodded, licking your lips and focused your energy to your hands. It was a small electrical discharge, Winter didn’t make a sound. “Stronger,” you obeyed and this time Winter made a small noise. It was no where near your full strength and knew it. He grabbed on your hair and pushed you against the wall.

    “Stop wasting my time,” he put his forearm against your throat. It was hard to get air into your lungs. “I will have him kill you, do you understand?” Kill me, you wanted to screamed, I want to die. You wanted this to be over.

    “Yes papa,” you whispered and he released you. You sucked in some much needed air and stepped back on top of the box. Placing your hands on the side of his head, you let out a shaky breath. “I’m sorry,” you mumbled and released the electricity. He fought it and you were impressed by that. Soon he couldn’t fight it and he screamed. He screamed and screamed and screamed.

    You jerked away and fell onto the floor with a thud. “Jesus Christ,” Kate said. The movie paused. “Are you okay?” You scrambled to your feet, starring at your friends and heart pounding.

    “Yeah,” you said, licking your lips. “Yeah, I’m great.” You chuckled. “Sorry, didn’t mean to startle everyone.” You felt your hands shake and you placed them behind your back out of view. But Yelena saw. The Black Widow stood up and took your hands in hers. You tensed at the contact, you didn’t want to hurt her.

    “A nightmare?” She questioned, sitting you back down on the couch. You nodded. “Do you want to talk about it?” You shook your head. “Okay, then we can sit here until you are okay.” “I’m fine,” you didn’t believe the lie you told your friends.

    “Your shaking,” America whispered. You weren’t even aware you were.

    “Oh,” you spoke softly.

    “It’s okay, bud. We’ll sit here and watch the movie until you feel better.” You nodded as America continued the movie. As the movie played, the visual affects and funny dialogue helped your body relax. But in the back of your mind, all you felt was fear. When your body stopped shaking, you excused yourself to your room. You took a cold shower to wash away the dream and changed into a long sleeve shirt and sleep shorts. But you knew you weren’t going back to sleep. So you sat on your bed with a book in your hands. It was going to be a long night.

I tried to hide my tears but you knelt at my level, whipped away my tears, and reached into your pocket,” you mimicked this part of your story and held out your hand. “And when you opened it, there was a little rubber duck.” You showed him the duck.

    “What?” He questioned. “A rubber duck?” You smiled as the confusion was all over his face. “You’re making this up.”

    “I’m not,” you laughed at the deadpanned look he was giving you. “I swear! Look I was equally confused when you handed it to me. It was a rubbed duck just like this one,” you placed it on the counter between you and him. When your laughter died down, your smile became sad. “When I destroyed the faculty, I tried to find it but it was destroyed in the fire.” Finally, he took a sip of his no longer hot chocolate and picked up the duck with his flesh hand.

    “I need time to process the new memories,” you nodded.

    “I got nothing but time,” you said. “I’ll be here when you’re ready.”

    “Thank you,” he smiled. “For the talk and the hot chocolate.”

    “Of course,” you cleaned your mug. “Try to get some sleep. Good night.” “Night, doll,” he said. But you knew sleep wasn’t going to be an option for you. Every time you closed your eyes, your past played out in front of you. It was like Yelena said, there was a scab over a wound you had and although it was healing every time you rubbed against it it hurt more. Your feet led you to the training area, and the sound of your footsteps echoed against the quiet room. It was weird being here when no one else was here. You could do anything without the watchful eyes of the Avengers. You could train, lift some weights, or go for a run. Instead, you walked over to the machine Tony and Bruce built you. It was in the corner of the room, a white sheet over it that you took off and stared at the machine. Fingertips tracing every part of it. With a sigh, you took a few steps from it and faced the machine. You closed your eyes and let out a few deep breaths. When you open your eyes, you let out of stream of electricity towards the target. You only cut it off when the batteries were full but it wasn’t enough. So you replaced the full batteries with empty ones and did it again. And again. And again. Until your chest was heaving and your legs felt like jello. A yawn escaped your lips and you took it as your sign to be done. You recovered the machine and took a long walk back to your room.

    As soon as your head hit the pillow, you fell into a dreamless sleep.

    Chapter 26

Easy. “Please.” Thor looked down at the floor; you could see his internal debate. You felt a little bad that you were the reason behind it.

    “I’ll show you how to create it before I throw it at you.” You nodded. It was a good compromise. You learned that electricity and lightning were related phenomena but differed in source, scale, and characteristics. Electricity was the term used to describe the flow of electric charge, while lightning resulted from the buildup and discharge of electrical energy in the atmosphere. Thor told you had to concentrate on the discharge. It was still a lot harder than you thought. No matter how many times you mimicked Thor’s movement, you couldn’t get it. Still, sweat poured down your back. “Look, Lady Maximoff jr, I don’t expect you to get it today,” Thor said. “It would be impressive.” You stood up straighter, allowing more air to get into your lungs.

It felt different than the normal feeling. “One more time,” you repeated and closed your eyes, hands outstretched. You felt a gentle vibration fill the air, almost like a heartbeat. You opened your eyes at the scent of petrichor mingled with the crackling energy that emanated from your fingertips. You looked at Thor, who had a smile and nodded. The air seemed to hum with anticipation as sparks of electricity danced along your fingertips. With a swift motion, you brought your hands together, and a radiant glow enveloped you. Threads of lightning began to coil and weave around you. You felt the energy reach its peak, and you redirected the coursing energy toward Thor. Bolts of lightning erupted from your fingertips, arcing and weaving through the air.

    Thor caught the energy quickly and, without thinking, redirected it back to you. Your right hand was still stretched, and the lightning connected with your fingertips. Your body seemed to pulse with energy as you felt the lightning travel through your body. It should have scared you, but you felt oddly calm as you guided the electrifying force away from your body and back to God. The room fell silent once Mjolnir collected the lightning and contained the energy. Your chest was heaving at the sudden display of power you weren’t sure came from. “Lady Maximoff, Lady Romanoff,” you turned around to see Natasha and Wanda walking over to you. The room was filled with your friends, but they seemed to be watching you and Thor rather than focusing on what they were doing. Even the couple had a look of surprise on their face. “Your daughter is a quick learner and powerful.” The way he called you their daughter made you feel warm. You ran over to them, legs feeling a little wobbly, but you hugged Natasha. She smiled at you, whipping your sweaty hair out of your face. “That she is,” she said. “Where did you learn how to do that?” You shrugged, stretching out your arms and legs.

    It seemed second nature for you to sit back and watch. You weren’t trying to be rude but liked understanding how everyone fit in a group. Then, you could see where you fit into it. You should have said no, called it off, and tried again later in the day. But you were stubborn, and you wanted to prove to yourself that you could do it. The first sets went well. You dogged the clay plates and destroyed them before they hit the ground. Until you plant your foot and your ankle gave out. Your body crumbled to the ground.

Honestly, you weren’t sure. Maria, Steve, Natasha, and Wanda all came up to you and said you weren’t required to. However, that would leave your friends down one member. But you weren’t a fighter. Just a girl gifted with a unique gift. “Not sure,” you said. “I’m leaning towards probably not.” To your surprise, there was no disappointment or anger in her eyes. “That’s totally fine! Hell your not even an Avenger,” America smiled. “We haven’t watched Avatar in forever. Do you wanna watch a few episodes before dinner?” One of the best things about America was that she knew when to pivot.

    “Yes, I do!” You said. “Your room, my room, or the couch?”

    “Your room,” she said. “It’s my favorite place in the tower.” America placed the ice pack in the sink. She grabbed a thing of popcorn and placed it in the microwave. Next, she grabbed the strawberries Wanda had cut up. It was giving you major deja vu from the night you first met her.

    “Why is it your favorite place?” You finally asked. America’s smile got bigger. “Because it’s your room,” she said. “Everything in their reminds me of you.”

    Chapter 33

    Day two followed the same format as the first day. A physical training followed by a rather explosive talk with Tony and Bruce. Luckily, no one was hurt. Then it was pizza for lunch, and you beat Shang-Chi in Just Dance. Instead of another lesson, they allowed you time to brainstorm a battle strategy for the fight at the end of the week.

    The mock battle was going to be a three-phase attack. The first was going to be a capture- based system. The longer you were in a given area, the more points you would receive. The points needed to capture would change from time to time.

    The second chase would be a tag. One person on each team would be randomly selected as ‘it’. The objective would be to protect your teammate while also attacking the enemy. And finally, a free-for-all battle. Points would be given based on the attack and on how well you communicate and work together. Each member would receive a vest that would act as a target to calculate how many times they’ve been ‘hit.’ Maria said they would tell you who would be participating before the fight. It only seemed fair in your eyes since they knew who would be fighting for the Young Avengers. Well, you were still a question mark. In your opinion, nothing really came from that brainstorming session. There was a lot of back-and-forth with no real ideas. Finally, Peter put on Mario Kart and handed out controllers.

    By the third day, your body had grown accustomed to the demanding schedule, and you were the first one in the gym for a training session with Okoye. Besides your weapon lesson with Natasha and Val, you were very excited for this. The fighting style of the Dora Milaje looked so cool to you, and it was a group of all-women warriors. They were badass! You stretched your arms above your head and felt your shoulders pop. Thankfully, they gave you tomorrow off for the physical aspect. You heard it was Natasha and Helen who pushed for the break. Recovery was just as important as working out. Sighing, you lie on the ground with your hands behind your head. Your eyes closed. Recently, there was a bit growing in your stomach. You could feel it more in recent days than in others. Right now, it was shimmering and wanted to escape. The palms of your hands pushed down onto the gym floor. A small surge of electricity left your palm and traveled across the tower. Suddenly, a wave of information entered your mind. You knew how many phones were in the tower and who was using them. If you thought hard enough, you could see what Cassie was Googling. Sam was watching a baseball game. Riri was listening to music, and Tony and Bruce were coding in the lab. All the technology, whether used or not, was collected in your mind. The energy shifted, and you felt your friends walking towards the gym. You sat up slowly. The last thing you needed was for your friends to see you doing something weird and not be able to explain it.

    Something was changing within you. You felt it when training with Thor, in Russian with Melina, and the past few days, as if you were going stronger at an alarming rate. What Vision said kept nagging at you - they weren’t sure if you were safe. What was coming after you that would need secret meetings?

    “Hey,” Kamala said, running over to you. You put a smile on your face. “I barely survived your mom,” she joked and put her arm around your shoulders. “How do you think I’ll do with the Dora Milaje?” You laughed.

    “She’s probably gonna lay you flat on your ass,” you joked. Kamala gasped playfully. Besides, you were safe as long as you were with the Avengers.

    ───────── ౨ ৎ ───────── “Again,” Okoye ordered. There was sweat pouring down your back. Your arms felt like jello from holding the fake wooden spear. Honestly, you wanted to give in and call it quits, but Okoye said to go again, so you followed the order.

    At the beginning of the class, she taught you the footwork, then she added the spear. After a short water break, she randomly assigned you a partner. Somehow, you weren’t sure how random it was when you saw Shuri walk over to the general and speak in a language you weren’t familiar with yet. But Okoye glanced at you, then back at the princess. You were partnered with Shuri after the break.

    The problem was that you held back your punches. The last thing you wanted was to hurt the king of Wakanda’s sibling and have the whole Dora Milaje after you. When you landed on your ass for the third time, Shuri huffed and extended her hand to help you up. “Why are you holding back?” She questioned. “I have seen you hit Yelena harder.” You glanced at the Blonde Black Widow, who was facing off against Riri.

    “Yeah, well, Yelena isn’t a sibling to a king and has an army at her beck and call,” You mumbled and dusted yourself off. Shuri laughed, bent down to pick up your spear, and handed it to you.

    “Please, my brother would probably thank you for knocking me around,” you smiled at her confession. “Train as if this is a real battle,” she told. “It is the only way to get better.” You nodded, twisting the wood in your hands.

    “Let’s go again, yeah?” Shuri.

    “Bring it.” Okoye believed footwork was the most important thing. Regardless of how hard you struck, if your feet weren’t strong, the weapon in your hand would fall. It seemed a few of your friends were struggling with that concept as they landed on their butts when Okoye tested their base.

    You lunged first. Shuri pivoted aside, her back foot moving in a half-moon shape. Her spear tapped you in the ribs lightly. Not meant to harm you but to correct you. There was a playful smile on her face.

    Sighing, you closed your eyes and twisted the wood in your hands. Okoye said the earth was your ally. Respect it. Lean into it. When you reopened your eyes, you moved again. Slower, you shifted your weight and advanced in small and more deliberate steps. Shuri’s smile grew as she mirrored your movements.

    This time, Shuri moved first, but you were ready for her. You felt the shuttle change in her heartbeat when she was going to strike. Your spear clashed with hers. Her wrist twisted, trying to trap your weapon, but you stepped back. Diagonally instead of straight to break the line of attack, instead of retreating.

    Good, you thought, before she would have knocked you down. But Shuri moved again; she feinted high, but you did not fall for it. She shifted left and closed the distance - her spear caught your ankle. Your balance wavered, but you remained standing.

    You recovered, ignoring the beads of sweat pouring down your back. When you moved, you took a page from Shuri’s book. You glanced at her feet and pivoted sharply, stepping outside her stance. Again, your spear’s locked, but you stepped in and not back. At the same time, the butt of your spear hooked behind Shuri’s ankle. Your shoulder bumped into her chest. Shuri tried to recover, but her weight went back, and she hit the ground. Hard. You stood above her with your spear leveled. A smile now on your face as you finally got the better of her. You mirrored her action and extended your hand to her. “Not bad,” she said as she stood up. “You are a fast learner.”

    “Is that a good thing or a bad thing?” You questioned and caught the water bottle she threw at you. Her silence lasted a second, maybe two, as you allowed the cool water to soothe your dry throat.

    “Both,” you answered. You put the cap on the bottle and looked at your friend. Her head was tilted to the side as she thought more. “I would hate to be on your bad side.” You laughed. “Good thing we don’t have to worry about that,” you said. “Wanna go again?” “You are on!”

    ───────── ౨ ৎ ───────── The sound of your name coming from the general froze you to your spot. “Come here for a second.” You turned around.

    “I’ll make sure Shang-Chi doesn’t eat your burger.” America teased. Words were stuck in your throat, so you could only manage a nod as you walked back to Okoye. You passed Shuri. Your friend had a knowing smile on her face, and she pumped her shoulder against yours. Finally, it was just you and her. The silence was loud. You wondered if she could hear the pounding of your heart against your ribs. “Why do you look frightened, child?” She questioned.

    “Because I have no idea what you are going to say to me.” Maybe Shuri was wrong, and you were about to be killed for hurting her. At least you’ve lived a good life with the short time you were with the Avengers. Okoye scuffed.

    “So I have been told. Shuri has grown quite fond of you,” she took a few steps to shorten the distance between you and her. “I would like to teach you something for your next lesson.” “Of course, General, it would be an honor.” To your surprise, she rolled her eyes at you. “Lose the title. It’s Okoye.” Right. Cool. First name based on the general of the Dora Milaje. No big deal.

    “Okoye,” you said her name softly. Like it was a secret between you and her. “Shall we begin? There is a burger and some fries with my name on it.” Okoye laughed quietly with a shake of her head.

    “Come sit,” she said, crossing her legs, and you sat in front of her. “Close your eyes and breathe.” You did as you were told. You weren’t expecting to mediate with Okoye, but you learned early on in life to never judge a book by their cover. “Being a warrior is not all about your physical strength; your mental strength is just as important.” You almost laughed. Your mental health was not great.

    All of your life, you were told you were a mistake, evil, and the devil was the reason you were born. The scars you had ran deep. They were just beginning to heal. You twisted your hands in your leggings. “Breathe through it, child,” Okoye said. “Deep breaths in and slowly let it out.” You inhaled and exhaled slowly. The general waited until your hands relaxed. “Good,” she praised. “The goal of today is not to be perfect but for you to have the building blocks so you can practice this without me.” You nodded.

    “I am going to teach you a skill called identity anchoring. Mastering this skill is to make your sense of self stable so it doesn’t fracture under guilt or manipulation,” you opened one eye at her. “Did I tell you to open your eyes?” You shut it immediately.

    “No,” you said. “I just-” you huffed, but continued, “My sense of self has been destroyed over the years. I don’t really know who I am.”

    “Then let’s find it together. I am going to say some phrases, and you are going to think of the answer before repeating the phrase back to me.” That seemed easy enough. “Do you understand?”

    “Yes, general.”

    “Let us begin,” she paused, then continued. “What do I protect?”

    What do I protect? That was a loaded question. Before the Avengers, you only had yourself, and you tried so hard to protect yourself from the evils of the world that wanted to harm you. Now you have a family. You had Natasha and Wanda, who have shown you unconditional love. You had Yelena, who has taught you ways to protect yourself and others. Everyone in this tower and extending to Iowa, Russia, and a few of the people you’ve met in the city, you would do anything to protect them.

    “What do I protect?” You finally spoke back.

    “What do I refuse to become?” She asked.

    Your father. That man at HYDRA. The group of bullies that tormented you on the streets. You were never going to hurt the people you loved. “What do I refuse to become?” You said much sooner than the first statement.

    “Who do I stand for when no one is watching?” That one made you pause. Your eyebrows pinched together. Maybe you could say Natasha. Wanda? But those answers weren’t good enough for you. They were new additions to your life. For the longest time, you were alone. You fought to survive every single day because no one was there to fight for you. That was your answer. You stood for yourself.

    “Who do I stand for when no one is watching?”

    “Good,” she praised again. “I am going to give you a phrase that combines these questions into one phrase. Repeat it after me once you feel emboldened: I am the shield. I do not break.”

    Your mind went to Steve’s shield. Made out of vibranium from Okoye’s home. It was nearly indestructible, protecting him and the team.

    Gods, you wished you had a shield like that when you were a kid. It would have taken the punches, the hurtful words, and the pain inflicted on you. But no. You took them. You were the shield for your heart, and you kept going. You kept fighting. Against all odds, you survived.

    You inhaled deeply and exhaled slowly. In your mind, you were no longer in the gym on the floor with Okoye, but back at the home you grew up in. His screams were loud enough to be heard over the TV as your mother and brother watched on. You walked past them, down the stairs to the basement, and saw your younger self being belittled by someone who called himself a man.

    Jonathan’s words were muffled. You couldn’t remember what he was yelling. This was a familiar scene you found yourself in. You walked with a purpose and stood between him and your younger self. Your hands went to her ears to block Johnathan’s poisonous words. You closed your eyes and became the protector for your younger self that no one would be. “I am the shield,” you said. “And I do not break.” There was a warmth that filled your body. You felt calmer. More confident and free.

    Her voice broke the silence. “After this get-together, you should come visit Wakanda.” You opened your eyes and watched her stand up. She offered you her hand to help you up. “We can do more training there. You would enjoy Shuri’s lab.”

    “Training?” You finally asked once your brain caught up with her words. “With you and the rest of the Dora Milaje.” Okoye nodded.

    “As long as your mothers approve.”

    “I will get them to approve.” She chuckled.

    “You are stubborn, child.” You followed her to the door. You were so excited to eat. You hoped they ordered the milkshake you requested. Mhm. Fires and milkshakes were a great combo.

    “Is that a good thing or a bad thing?” You asked. It was a deja vu moment with Shuri. “Being stubborn can be a good thing. Being stubborn can be a bad thing,” you groaned because she refused to give you a clear answer. “It just depends on how you use it.” ───────── ౨ ৎ ───────── What Okoye said wrapped around you all day and kept repeating in your head. It was why you were awake. Even though you were exhausted from the second part of the day. Steve and Maria were back training for your gym session. They were just as brutal as the first time around.

    Sighing, your feet took you to Overwatch with your notebook and pencil tucked underneath your arm. You felt a little bad for swiping Tony’s access card. He would be the only one to notice it was missing. Besides, you could have it returned by the morning. You slide the card through the reader and open the door. “Miss. Y/n,” FRIDAY’s voice startled you as you walked in and closed the door behind you. “Do the others know you are here?” “No FRIDAY,” you sat down in the chair that faced the many screens. You felt powerful. “Are you going to tell on me?” You began to spin in the chair. The AI was quiet for a beat. “Just be careful, Miss,” the AI said. “I will be monitoring your work. If I deem it too dangerous, I will be alerting the team.” You smiled, stopped spinning, and cracked your fingers as you faced the screens.

    “Sounds good FRIDAY.” You were just doing research on the list of missing people. How hard could it be?

    ───────── ౨ ৎ ───────── Impossible apparently. It was like these people disappeared. Facial recognition had nothing. No street camera. No surveillance video on ATMS. Nothing in the background in the photos on social media. Nothing. These people had stories, and now they were gone like ghosts. “I don’t understand,” you squeezed the stress ball. “With all the cameras, all the technology, all the eyes, how do people just disappear?” You asked the empty space. The quiet was your answer.

    You leaned against the table. With your head swimming with more questions than answers, his voice scared you. “Are you giving up already?” You spun around and threw the stress ball at Nick. The man caught it easily. “Seriously, a stress ball as a weapon.” You rolled your eyes.

    “Sorry, I misplaced my ninja stars.” You caught the ball when he threw it back at you. “Didn’t know there was another door in here.”

    “Most don’t,” he answered, moving silently into the room. “Heard you were participating in this little party.” You nodded. “Are you going to fight at the end of the week?” “Not sure yet,” you turned your attention back to the screens. Pictures of faces danced back at you. Like they were mocking you. “Besides, I’m trying to figure out this mess.” You gestured to the screens and crossed your arms.

    “Can’t see why you can’t do both,” you heard him sit down in the rolling chair you pushed away. You turned around to face him. His hands were folded together under his chin. You wondered if he had any other clothes in his closet.

    “What do you want, Nick?” You questioned.

    “Fury,” you held your glare with the man. “We haven’t seen each other in a while. I thought I’d stop by and see the talk of the tower.” The way he said that made your skin prickle. The hairs on the back of your neck. You rubbed it subconsciously. It reminded you of the way Carlos said people were talking about you.

    You hated it. Being the center of attention was bad. It meant bruises, yelling, and promises that were broken and never kept. “I’m just me,” you said. “Nothing special.” The man huffed out a laugh.

    “Someone who is ‘nothing special,’” he added the quotes around the word. “Wouldn’t be looking into people who are missing that have no connection to you or coming up with a battle strategy for your friends when you aren’t fighting.”

    “Creepy.” You mumbled.

You shrugged. “The last time I trusted someone, I lost it.” Weird. But he answered your question, and you began to pace again.

The more you thought about it, the more your head began to spin and ache. Then you found him, deep in your memories. It was small, insignificant really. You were being brought back to your cell when you heard voices. You turned to look into the room they were coming from. There were two men talking with the man you called Papa. I know it’s probably not the answer you wanted.” “Sometimes,” he hit a few more keys, and it changed to a fingerprint identification. He gestured for you. You placed your finger on the scanner on the keyboard, and you were added to the system. “We don’t have to like the answer. We just have to accept it.” Audio files now were staring back at you.

We call it the veil,” he turned to face you. “Also, creatures like the Skrull can change who they look like.” “Skrull,” you mumbled. Sam said Carlos was a Skrull. So the man you spoke with wasn’t what he normally looked like. Maybe you should have paid more attention to Carol’s lesson. “But why hide your features?” You took a few steps towards the man. “Don’t these people want to be found?”

    “Maybe they don’t.”

    “So what?” you threw your arms to the side. “I should just give up.”

    “I didn’t say that,” he chuckled as you huffed in frustration. “All I’m saying is the easiest answer might be the one staring at you in the face. You might not like it, but that’s the way it is.”

    “That’s bullshit.”

    “That’s life,” he turned back around to leave. “Don’t stay up here all night, kid, or I’ll have to tell Natasha about our nightly chat.”

    “Night, Nick,” you waved at the man. When he closed the door, you fell back into the chair and groaned. Yeah, that could be the easiest answer. But you weren’t going to accept that. These people were in trouble. You could feel it, deep within your bones. You weren’t going to stop until you found them.

    Chapter 34

    Gods above you were tired. Maybe because you spent way too long in Overwatch the night before or maybe Vision’s history lesson was just that boring, but day four was dragging on. You almost fell asleep at the shooting range while Maria was going over gun safety. Now you were listening to the android go on and on about the history of the infinity stones. On any given day, you’d love to listen about how the fabric of the universe was woven together, but you were barely holding it together.

The only way Yelena was remotely paying attention was because she was playing with Kate’s hand, and the other had a switchblade. She was opening and closing it so quickly, you were sure she was going to cut herself. You had no idea how you were going to get through the second half of the day. You think Wanda was next or maybe Thor.

The room cleared out in 10 seconds. ───────── ౨ ৎ ───────── The only thing you grabbed from your room was a bag that had your phone, wallet, and camera. You weren’t allowed to watch TV, but you could hear the 30 minutes Caleb was allowed to. Even when you were at the shelters, kids would crowd around it when they brought it out. The bright colors scared you. However, you got used to it over time. You still preferred books instead.

You were the author, and the people around you became your characters. You gave them backstories, superpowers, and quests to go on while you people watched from a safe distance.

You heard Yelena mumble, ‘kiss ass.’ “Maybe we should let a surprise be a surprise.” “This was my idea,” Wanda said from the passenger seat and turned to face the group. “Sometimes the best teaching tool is to have fun.”

    Chapter 37

    There had to be some truth to your lie, or it wasn’t going to convince Wanda or you. Yelena coached you on how to avoid her powers. Repetition.     “You know,” America said slowly. “Natasha and Wanda gave me a long talk before the party.” Your eyebrow raised to your hairline.

    “Are you okay? Did they threaten you?” America laughed.

    “No, they didn’t try to scare me away.” Good. You would never have forgiven them if they had done such a thing. “They just made me promise a few things.”

    “Like?” You questioned. America smirked.

    “My lips are sealed.” She used her free hand to zip her lips. You rolled your eyes. “You are so annoying.” The girl laughed with her head tilted back.

    “I am,” she teased. “Unfortunately, you are stuck with me.”

    “Yeah,” you smiled fondly. “I am.” You leaned across the table. She met you and your lips touched. You were never going to get used to the feeling of her lips against yours. She made your stomach drop. All of the noise from the party disappeared. All that mattered was her. Until someone cleared their throat and pulled away from her, you stared at Yelena. “Am I interrupting?” She teased.

    “Yes,” you deadpanned. “You are.”

    “Then don’t have your tongue down each other’s throat in a public space.” You felt America tense up, but you held the Blonde’s glare. “Come on, you two love birds. The party is waiting for you.” America sighed, stood up from the table, and kissed your cheek. “I’ll catch up with you.” You promised. America nodded and left you alone with Yelena. “So,” the blonde walked closer to you. “Do your mamas know about this development between you and America?”

    “Technically,” you answered slowly. “Natasha talked to me yesterday, and they both gave her a shovel talk. What they said to her is a mystery to me.” Yelena hummed and crossed her arms.

    “Just to be clear, if she hurts you, I will hurt her.” You shook your head. “No, you won’t,” you stood up from the chair. The blonde raised her eyebrows. “Regardless of what happens between her and me, if you hurt her, I won’t speak to you again.” Yelena scoffed. “And you’ve known her longer.”

    “You’re my niece.”

    “I know.” It made you feel all warm inside when Yelena brought up your new relationship. “Still correct on what I said. Besides, I’m not worried about her hurting me.” Yelena smiled and threw her arm around your shoulders.

    “Happy for you.” Yeah, you were happy too.

    No one questioned the way you sat too close to America with her hand in yours. It seemed second nature for the two of you. Tomorrow coming was bittersweet. It marked the end of the week-long initiative, and you survived. But it meant all your friends were leaving. Shuri was already on her way back to Wakanda with Okoye. Cassie and Shang-Chi were leaving early in the morning to go back to California. Riri was going back to school, and the rest of the team would be spread across the world to save the day.

    This was the last night with all your friends. “I’d like to make a toast,” Steve said and stood up with a beer in his hands.

    “I’m gonna take notes,” Kate pretended to flip open a notepad. “I have to work on my speeches.”

    “Whatever you said clearly worked,” Natasha said, sipping on her drink. Yelena used both her hands to squeeze Kate’s cheeks. The archer pushed her away, and you could make out the blush on her cheeks.

    “Anyways,” Steve laughed. “Although this week was to get all of you ready to take over when we step down. I think this week cemented the idea all of us already knew: you are all capable heroes.”

    “Aw, he’s getting sentimental.” Maria teased. You looked over your shoulder. The Deputy Director winked at you.

    “I do not know why I put up with you all.” He said, putting his beer to his temple. “Because you love us!” Tony cheered. You cupped your mouth and cheered. That started a chorus of shouts and cheers. Some people were stomping their feet. Others were clapping their hands. Shang-Chi put his fingers to his mouth and whistled. When the noise finally died down, you noticed a glimmer in Steve’s eyes. The man was desperately holding back tears. “I do,” he said. “I love all of you.” The tone shifted. Everyone seemed to sit up a little straighter. “And I think I wouldn’t want to fight alongside anyone else, although the future is uncertain. As long as we are together, there is no obstacle we can not face. Cheers!” “Cheers!” Everyone raised their drink. Slowly, you sipped on the root beer float that Natasha made.

    ‘The stones,’ Nick said. ‘Rarely warn us of something good.’

    You were going to be the exception. You were going to make sure of it. End Notes I hope you all enjoyed chapter 1 and it makes you excited for the rest of the story! Kudos and comments are always appreciate.
